# sillajuku

Every record you've loved — rated, cataloged, and remembered. A music platform for listeners with taste.

**Stack:** Next.js 14 (App Router) · Swift/SwiftUI (iOS) · Supabase (auth + database) · Spotify API · Tailwind CSS

**Monorepo:** `apps/web` (Next.js) · `apps/ios` (Swift — in progress) · `packages/shared` (TypeScript types)

---

## ⚠️ Current state (2026-07-15)

Features shipped as of 2026-06-08: Daily Question, preferred streaming platform, **Collections** (custom playlists, renamed) with foldable panel and per-add destination picker, Bayesian Silla Score, Discovery/Adventurousness slider. See SESSIONS.md for details. **2026-06-10:** `backfill:embeddings` ✅ complete — all ~347k non-single releases have Jina v3 embeddings. HNSW index ✅ rebuilt (2026-06-09). Hybrid semantic search ready — deploy to Vercel to activate. **2026-06-11:** Performance overhaul — homepage blank-screen fix (Suspense streaming), GIN trigram index on `releases.genres` (eliminates IO-exhausting full-table scans; required **Supabase Pro** upgrade to build — free tier Disk IO Budget was exhausted), Redis caching on album page ranking badges (5-min TTL, 10–15 queries → 1 cache hit), profile page `auth.admin.listUsers(1000)` → targeted SQL function, query limits on category-resolver and canon-suggestions. New live search dropdown: `SearchBar` component + `/api/search/suggest` endpoint (prefix match, no Jina, 10-min Redis cache) — near-instant after first query. **2026-06-14:** Local QA pass complete — 4 bugs found and fixed (ISSUE-002–006). Logo updated to transparent background. Browser language detection added (Accept-Language header + navigator.language). Onboarding improved: sidebar/footer/listen-later hidden, logo non-clickable, genre pills sorted by DB frequency, Apple Music added as streaming option (requires migration `20260615000000`), essentials prioritize Albums > EPs > Singles. **2026-06-16 (mobile):** Expo app brought up on Windows/Expo Go (fixed an SDK-56-vs-54 dependency skew in `apps/mobile/package.json`). Mobile design pass started — device-language i18n (`apps/mobile/lib/i18n/`, follows the phone's language, en/ko), login redesign (non-scroll + flower-mark logo), home navbar removed, unified `ScreenHeader` across the four tab screens. `backfill:tracklists` ✅ complete (107,778 / 115,604 = 93%). See SESSIONS.md (2026-06-16) for details + known issues. **2026-06-17 (architecture pivot):** React Native (`apps/mobile`) retired and deleted. iOS app rebuilding from scratch in Swift/SwiftUI (`apps/ios/` — Mac session). New feature decisions: OAuth-only login (Spotify recommended, Apple, Google — no email/password), simplified onboarding (name + username + rating mode + notifications; Google adds genre step), Essentials feature removed from entire app, Instinct rating mode added (pairwise comparison / Elo). See `WEB_PARITY.md` for full spec of changes to mirror on web. Two parallel tracks: Mac builds iOS app; Windows builds Elo system, DB migrations, `/api/rate/compare` endpoint, removes essentials from web, updates Spotify OAuth scopes. **2026-06-17 (session 3, Windows):** all 5 web-prep tasks built — Instinct migration (`20260617000000`, ⏳ apply via SQL editor), `lib/elo.ts`, `/api/rate/compare`, essentials removed (ProfilePanel + AlbumActions + onboarding `StepAlbums`; `Essentials.tsx`/`Pick5Modal.tsx` deleted; `pinned_albums` left unused), Spotify login scopes (`user-top-read` + `user-read-recently-played`). Typecheck clean. Remaining web parity (onboarding rewrite, Instinct album-page UI, `/rate` session, settings toggle, i18n) still open — see START HERE. **2026-06-18 (iOS tab screens):** All 5 tab screens built — `HomeView` (parallel genre carousels from `recommendable_releases`, `withTaskGroup`), `SearchView` (debounced search with 3-column grid), `RankingsView` (ranking categories list), `ActivityView` (global ratings feed with cover + score + timestamp), `ProfileView` (stats, rating mode badge, 3-column grid with score overlay, sign out). Shared: `AlbumCard` component, `Release` + `RankingCategory` models, `Date.relativeTimeString` helper. Simulator still blocked on 8GB RAM — use Xcode Previews (`#Preview` on each screen) to test without the full simulator. **2026-06-18 (session 2 — album detail + physical device):** `AlbumDetailView` built — full-width cover hero, title/artist/release type chip/year, community stats (avg ★ + count), half-star rating widget (tap to rate, tap same to clear, writes to `ratings` via upsert), tracklist section (from `tracklist` JSONB column — fetched separately on detail load, MM:SS duration). `TrackItem` model + `StarRatingView` component added. `Release` extended with `Hashable`, `tracklist`, `totalTracks`. `NavigationLink(value:)` wired into every tappable card: `HomeView` carousels, `SearchView` grid, `ProfileView` ratings grid, `ActivityView` list rows. Debug build config updated to `CODE_SIGN_STYLE = Automatic`, team `GGJ5HX3A4M` — app can now be deployed to a physical iPhone via Xcode. **2026-06-18 (iOS session 3 — UI polish + dark mode):** Auth screen polished — two oversized decorative flowers (top-right 140% screen width / bottom-left 100%, opacity 0.09, cut at physical screen edges via `ignoresSafeArea`), legal text pinned to bottom in a separate ZStack layer, slogan rendered as `Color.sjInk.opacity(0.6)`. > **✅ RESOLVED (2026-09-21, Mac) — Apple-linking saga fully closed, confirmed live server-side, not just by the UI.** Three distinct real causes, each found and fixed in turn: (1) expired Supabase Apple OAuth secret (6-month Apple max lifetime), (2) `com.sillajuku.app` was only ever registered as an App ID, never a proper Services ID (which Sign-in-with-Apple's web/OAuth flow structurally requires) — created `com.sillajuku.app.signin` with the right Domains/Return URLs, (3) the user's real Apple ID was orphaned on an old, abandoned dev/test account (`@sillajuku`, created 2026-07-22) that `auth.admin.deleteUser()` apparently didn't clean up an identity row for — found via direct SQL (an `admin.listUsers()` scan gave a false "nothing here"), confirmed it was genuinely the user's own account before deleting, then verified `app_metadata.providers` server-side shows `["spotify","apple"]` after reconnecting. **Open question for later, not investigated further**: why Apple-linked test accounts leave orphaned `auth.identities` rows on deletion when Spotify/Google ones (tested extensively this same session) don't.
>
> Along the way, added Sentry visibility to three more previously-silent failure points this thread's diagnosis needed: `ConnectedAccountsViewModel.link()`, `.load()`, and `sillajukuApp.swift`'s `onOpenURL` code-exchange step — all committed.
> Chain: expired Supabase Apple secret (6-month Apple max lifetime) → regenerated, but hit a NEW, later error ("Signup not completed" from `appleid.apple.com` itself) → root-caused to `com.sillajuku.app` only ever being registered as an **App ID** (native sign-in), never a genuine **Services ID** — the separate identifier type Sign-in-with-Apple's web/OAuth flow structurally requires, with its own Domains/Return URLs config, which this project never had reason to create before. Walked the user through creating one (`com.sillajuku.app.signin`) with Return URL set to **Supabase's own callback** (`https://mmbptchpetwdievhrdsj.supabase.co/auth/v1/callback` — Supabase brokers the Apple exchange before redirecting back to the app, so that's the URL Apple actually needed registered), regenerated the JWT secret for the new Services ID, updated both Client ID and Secret Key in Supabase. Re-test: flow visually completed (Face ID, no error) but the identity still never landed server-side (confirmed via direct DB check) — `link()` itself didn't throw this time, meaning the actual OAuth code exchange was failing silently one step later. Found it: `sillajukuApp.swift`'s `.onOpenURL` handler had `Task { try? await supabase.auth.session(from: url) }` — the one remaining completely-unguarded step in the entire round trip. Added the same do/catch + `SentrySDK.capture(error:)` treatment as every other fix this session. Verified via clean simulator build — **BUILD SUCCEEDED**; the actual failure reason still needs one more live test to surface.
> `link()`'s Sentry capture (committed `ec45778`) revealed the real cause on retry: `Auth.AuthError: ... "Unsupported provider: missing OAuth secret"`. Linking Apple from Settings uses Supabase's generic web-redirect OAuth flow — a separate mechanism from the native Sign-in-with-Apple entitlement the app's initial signup uses, and the ONLY one that needs a Supabase-side Apple client secret. That secret is a JWT with a hard 6-month Apple-imposed maximum lifetime; user recalled setting it up "almost 6 months ago," matching. Regenerated it: wrote a local Node script (built-in `crypto` only) that signs the required ES256 JWT from a `.p8` key + Team ID + Key ID + Services ID, designed to run entirely on the user's machine. When asked to just hand over all 4 values, explained why the raw private key shouldn't be pasted into chat and offered a middle ground instead — the user gives the non-sensitive identifiers plus the key's file path, this session reads the key directly off disk and signs it, surfacing only the resulting bounded JWT back. User agreed; JWT generated, temp script deleted immediately after, pasted into Supabase. Separately: `ConnectedAccountsViewModel.load()` had the identical swallowed-`try?`-failure pattern `link()` had before this round — fixed the same way. Verified via clean simulator build — **BUILD SUCCEEDED**.
>

> **✅ RESOLVED (2026-09-21, Mac) — Diagnosed a web-login regression (landed on the "email confirmed" page instead of signing in) to a missing Supabase Dashboard config, NOT a code bug — no code change needed.** `app/(auth)/login/page.tsx` already passes its own explicit OAuth `redirectTo`, never relying on Site URL; `/auth/callback/page.tsx` has zero path that redirects to `/auth/confirmed`. Root cause: Supabase silently substitutes Site URL for any `redirectTo` not present in its separate "Redirect URLs" allow-list — that allow-list was missing `https://sillajuku.com/auth/callback` and `https://www.sillajuku.com/auth/callback` entirely, a latent gap invisible until Site URL started pointing at `/auth/confirmed` (the 2026-09-20 mobile-deep-link fix). **User confirmed both were genuinely missing and added them directly in the Dashboard.**

> **Full chain, for the historical record** (see SESSIONS.md 2026-09-21 for the blow-by-blow): cached-`appMetadata` gate → live `userIdentities()` check → DB check disproved that as the blocker → Sentry visibility added to previously-silent Spotify API calls → real `Codable` array-decode bug found+fixed → `userIdentities()` itself found silently swallowing errors → success-but-empty diagnostics → nested-field decode bug (locally-uploaded track) found+fixed → catalog-resolution never re-triggered after a background retry succeeds → "Recently Listened" waiting on an unrelated Discovery retry → still waiting on Discovery's first attempt (two rounds to fully decouple) → real design change to instant+tappable rows (user's explicit choice via `AskUserQuestion`) → resolved items not caching/upgrading on repeat taps → suspiciously-low item count led to discovering the Spotify access token had simply expired with no refresh path → first refresh attempt (client-side, client_id only) got a real Spotify 400, revealing this app is a confidential client needing a secret that must never live on-device → redirected through a new on-demand backend endpoint reusing the already-working `refresh-spotify-taste` cron's logic → confirmed working end-to-end live.
> New `apps/web/app/api/spotify/refresh-my-data/route.ts` — the on-demand, single-user version of the existing `refresh-spotify-taste` cron (Bearer-authenticated via the existing `getAuthedUserId()` helper, same pattern `app/api/spotify/auth/route.ts` uses), refreshes with Basic auth + `SPOTIFY_CLIENT_SECRET` server-side (deliberately duplicated from the cron rather than shared, matching that file's own stated reasoning), rewrites `profiles.spotify_artists`/`spotify_recently_played`/`spotify_data_updated_at`. iOS side: removed the broken client-side `SpotifyService.refreshAccessToken()`/`Config.spotifyClientId`/`SpotifyRefreshResponse` entirely rather than leaving them behind with a now-known-false assumption in their comments. Added `SpotifyService.refreshViaBackend()` (same Bearer-session-token auth pattern `WebAPI.swift` already uses), called from `loadSpotify()`'s Layer 3 when `validToken()` is nil — re-reads `loadArtistsFromDB()`/`loadRecentlyPlayedFromDB()` directly afterward (not gated on the caches already being non-empty, unlike Layer 2's own read) to pick up what the backend just wrote. Verified via clean simulator build (**BUILD SUCCEEDED**) and `tsc --noEmit` clean for the new route; the actual end-to-end round trip hasn't been exercised live yet.
> `SpotifyService.me (liveness check) failed: status=401` — `validToken()` had no recovery for an expired access token, so `loadSpotify()` never even attempted a live fetch; the user was just seeing an earlier successful fetch's leftover data. A refresh token was already being captured and saved at sign-in but never used anywhere on the Add tab. Built `SpotifyService.refreshAccessToken()`: POSTs directly to `accounts.spotify.com/api/token` (bypassing Supabase, which has no refresh path of its own for a provider token — only the initial sign-in exchange), needs the new `Config.spotifyClientId` (not a secret — same value as web's `.env.local` `SPOTIFY_CLIENT_ID`, safe to embed, same trust tier as the Supabase anon key/Sentry DSN already in that file). Updates cached access/refresh tokens (both locally and DB-persisted) on success; `validToken()` now calls it automatically instead of giving up when the liveness check fails. **One real uncertainty flagged to the user rather than assumed away**: this refresh call includes only `client_id`, correct for a PKCE/public-client Spotify app (the standard Supabase-brokered mobile setup, consistent with the `pkceGrantCodeExchange` error naming seen earlier this session) — but if Spotify's registered app needs a client secret instead, this specific call will fail with an auth error, which the existing Sentry logging will make immediately visible on the next test. Verified via clean simulator build — **BUILD SUCCEEDED**; not yet verified against a real expired-token scenario.
> `recentlyPlayed()` now captures a Sentry event whenever `albums.count < rawCount` (final deduped-album count lower than raw track-item count), not only when it hits zero. Deliberately covers both a real remaining decode-drop bug AND legitimate deduplication — this endpoint returns individual tracks, deduped to unique albums, so replaying the same single EP repeatedly would correctly show as 1 album even from several raw track entries, which isn't a bug. If no event fires on the next test and the DB still shows exactly 1 item, that's conclusive: Spotify's own response genuinely only had 1 track right now (matches the user's own earlier "haven't streamed recently"), nothing to fix. Verified via clean simulator build — **BUILD SUCCEEDED**. Diagnostic-only round, no behavior change.
> Root cause: nothing cached a resolution once made — `ResolvingAlbumView` re-resolved fresh on every push (its `@State` resets each navigation), and the row had no way to know an item had ever resolved before. Fixed with `DiscoveryViewModel.resolvedPreviewCache: [String: Release]`, fed from both resolution paths: `resolveCatalogMatches()` (used by the existing background `resolveRecentlyPlayedIfNeeded()`) now returns one `Release?` per input item instead of a compacted list, so the caller can zip originals against results and populate the cache — meaning most items are often already cached from the background job alone before the user even looks at the row. `ResolvingAlbumView` (now takes `discoveryVM` directly) also writes into the same cache on a successful on-tap resolution. `recentlyPlayedPreviewScroll` checks the cache per item: once resolved, renders the exact same `DiscoveryAlbumCard`/`NavigationLink(value: release)` every other section uses — rate button, type label, direct navigation, real context menu, zero loading screen — not just on the first tap but every time from then on. Verified via clean simulator build — **BUILD SUCCEEDED**.
> The remaining ~3s was the actual catalog-matching DB round trip (ILIKE + fuzzy RPC + artist-identity resolution) that "Your Top Artists" never needs (it just shows Spotify's raw data directly) — not fixable by re-sequencing, needed a real design change. Asked via `AskUserQuestion` rather than picking unilaterally; **user chose**: show the row immediately with Spotify's own raw track/cover data, every row tappable right away, resolve the catalog match live on tap with a loading state. Reused an exact existing precedent already shipped in this file: `spotifyArtistScroll` already does this for artists via `ArtistDestination(artistId: nil, ...)` + `ArtistPageView`'s own on-appear resolution. Built the album equivalent: `RecentlyPlayedDestination` (raw name/artist/imageUrl, no release id yet), `RecentlyPlayedPreviewCard` (simpler than `DiscoveryAlbumCard` — no rate button, unresolved), `recentlyPlayedPreviewScroll(_:)` (renders `discoveryVM.recentlyPlayed` directly, replacing the old catalog-gated `albumScroll` call), and `ResolvingAlbumView` (pushed instantly, shows Spotify's raw preview + spinner while `DiscoveryViewModel.fetchRelease(name:artist:)` — existing logic, reused as-is, changed `private`→`fileprivate` — resolves it; swaps to the real `AlbumDetailView` on success, or a plain "Not in our catalog yet" message on a genuine miss). `resolveRecentlyPlayedIfNeeded()`'s eager background resolution left unchanged — still needed for the Apple Music row, which keeps the old eager behavior for now (not part of what was reported broken, flagged as a likely future follow-up rather than changed unilaterally). Verified via clean simulator build — **BUILD SUCCEEDED**.
> `load()` previously ran `loadSpotify()`/`loadAppleMusic()`/Discovery's first attempt all in one `withTaskGroup`, which only completes once every member does — so "Recently Listened" (sequenced right after that group) was still gated behind Discovery's first attempt (~6s) regardless of the round-8 fix, since that fix only pulled the Discovery *retry* out, not the first attempt. Restructured into two fully independent `async let` chains: one for Spotify/Apple Music → `resolveRecentlyPlayedIfNeeded()`, one for Discovery + its own retry, with zero coupling between them. "Recently Listened" now starts resolving the instant `loadSpotify()`/`loadAppleMusic()` finish, regardless of Discovery's timing at all. Verified via clean simulator build — **BUILD SUCCEEDED**. Noted honestly to the user: some residual gap vs. "Your Top Artists" is structural, not a bug — that row renders raw Spotify data directly with no further async step, while "Recently Listened" inherently needs one extra catalog-matching round trip against our own database that the other row never does.
> The full chain that fixed the missing-data bug: cached-snapshot gate → live check (`606c0da`) → Sentry visibility (`d5f4dee`) → real `recentlyPlayed` decode bug found+fixed (`1dd5e01`) → `userIdentities()` silently swallowing errors, fixed (`32bcd18`) → success-but-empty diagnostics (`7af8665`) → nested-field decode bug found+fixed (`fe5bd7c`) → catalog-resolution never re-triggered after a background retry succeeds, fixed (`94bc487`) — user confirmed "Recently Listened" finally appeared. Follow-up: it took 10+ seconds, well after every other section — real UX bug, not the original missing-data one. Root cause, found immediately from `load()`'s own existing comments: `resolveRecentlyPlayedIfNeeded()` was sequenced strictly AFTER an unrelated one-shot Discovery retry that the code already documented as a real ~6s extra network round trip, even though it has no actual dependency on Discovery — only on `recentlyPlayed`/`appleMusicRecentlyPlayed`, both already populated earlier in `load()`. Fixed by running the Discovery retry and `resolveRecentlyPlayedIfNeeded()` concurrently in one `withTaskGroup` instead of sequentially — the same "merge unrelated sequential legs into one wave" pattern this function already used once before for its original ~12s cold-load fix. Verified via clean simulator build — **BUILD SUCCEEDED**.
> Chain so far: cached-snapshot gate → live check (`606c0da`) → DB check ruled that out → Sentry visibility added (`d5f4dee`) → real decode bug found+fixed (`1dd5e01`) → `userIdentities()` itself found silently swallowing errors, fixed (`32bcd18`) → success-but-empty diagnostics added (`7af8665`) → confirmed `top/artists` is a real, legitimate empty result (`short_term` needs recent activity — not a bug) but `recently-played`'s one real item still failed decode due to a nested field (most likely a locally-uploaded track's null album id), fixed with fully-lenient nested decoding (`fe5bd7c`). **This round**: re-tested, still 3 rows — but the DB now shows genuinely real data (`spotify_recently_played: [{"name":"MOHO","artistName":"dress",...}]`), and a direct catalog query confirms "MOHO - EP" by "dress & Raf Sandou" exists and would pass the existing artist-match gate. So the data is right and the catalog has it — the bug is downstream of `SpotifyService` entirely: `resolveRecentlyPlayedIfNeeded()` (which turns raw Spotify data into the catalog-matched releases the Add tab actually renders from) only ever runs once per `DiscoveryViewModel` instance, from `load()`/`refresh()` — **not** from `refreshSpotifyIfNeeded()`/`refreshAppleMusicIfNeeded()`, the functions that pick up a background retry succeeding *after* `load()` already ran and locked the guard with an empty result. Once real data finally arrives via a background retry (exactly what this session's whole run of Spotify fixes makes more likely), nothing ever re-triggers the catalog match — short of a full relaunch or manual pull-to-refresh. Fixed: both functions now reset the guard and re-resolve after their respective load call, matching `refresh()`'s existing pattern; fixed identically in both (Spotify and Apple Music) since they share the exact same gap. Verified via clean simulator build — **BUILD SUCCEEDED**. First fix in this whole saga that explains 100% of the observed evidence rather than a plausible mechanism.
> Checked Sentry directly after the user's re-test (per their ask to stop requiring manual screen reports) instead of asking what they saw. Only one new event: `loadSpotify exhausted retries: lastLinkedSpotify=true reachedLayer3=true`, fired twice ~1min apart. No capture from `validToken()`'s two failure paths, and none from `topArtists()`/`recentlyPlayed()`'s HTTP/decode-failure paths — meaning by elimination, `validToken()` is returning a real live token, and both live API calls are completing with a real 200 and decoding successfully. The only way `hasSpotifyData` still ends up false is if both calls are returning structurally valid, genuinely empty results — plausible for `top/artists` given its `time_range=short_term` param only returns data with ~4-week-recent listening activity (a real Spotify constraint, not a bug), less obviously explainable yet for `recently-played`. Added a success-but-empty capture to both: `top/artists` reports item count (0 expected if the short_term theory holds); `recently-played` reports raw item count AND individually-decoded count, to distinguish a genuinely empty response from every item failing decode from every item decoding but lacking a usable track+album. Verified via clean simulator build — **BUILD SUCCEEDED**. This round is a hypothesis-confirming instrument, not a fix yet — the likely actual fix (widening `top/artists`'s `time_range`, if that's what the next test confirms) is still pending.
> Every round of this bug has narrowed the search space with real evidence, not more guessing: cached-snapshot gate → live `userIdentities()` call (`606c0da`) → DB check disproved that as the blocker → added Sentry visibility to the previously-silent live API calls (`d5f4dee`) → found and fixed a genuine `Codable` array-decode bug in `recentlyPlayed()` (`1dd5e01`, confirmed via a real captured event: a 200 response with real data still failing to decode). Re-test after that fix produced the same symptom but with NO new Sentry events at all, not even the ones just added — meaning the loop is exhausting at a point *before* reaching the instrumented live API calls. The one remaining silent failure point: `let identities = (try? await supabase.auth.userIdentities()) ?? []` in `SearchView.swift`'s `loadSpotify()` — an error there is indistinguishable from a genuine "not linked" result, and was never logged either way, despite the DB already proving the account really is linked. Now a real `do/catch` capturing the actual error on the last attempt, plus a `defer`-based summary event (`lastLinkedSpotify`/`reachedLayer3`) that fires whenever the whole retry loop exhausts without populating `hasSpotifyData` — one event with the full picture instead of reconstructing it from scratch each round. Verified via clean simulator build — **BUILD SUCCEEDED**.
> Chain of misdiagnoses this thread, each ruled out with real evidence before moving to the next: (1) `appMetadata["providers"]` cached-snapshot gate → switched to a live `userIdentities()` call (`606c0da`) → bug recurred. (2) Direct DB check (service-role script) showed the account genuinely IS linked server-side — that wasn't it either. Added Sentry error capture to the previously-silent Spotify API call sites (`d5f4dee`) to stop guessing. (3) Got a read-only Sentry auth token from the user, found the iOS app's Sentry project (`sillajuku` org, `apple-ios` project — separate from web's), and the very first captured event was the answer: `SpotifyService.recently-played failed: status=200 body={"items":[{"track":...` — **a genuine 200 response with real track data, still failing to decode.** Root cause: Swift's `JSONDecoder` fails a `[SpotifyPlayItem]` array atomically the instant any ONE element throws (most likely a locally-uploaded file in the user's real recently-played history, which doesn't match this schema) — silently discarding all 49 other valid items along with it. Fixed with a lenient per-item decoding wrapper (`SpotifyRecentlyPlayedResponse.LenientPlayItem`) that drops only the item that fails instead of the whole batch. Verified via clean simulator build — **BUILD SUCCEEDED**. Separately flagged, not fixed: no equivalent failure was captured for `top/artists`, so "Your Top Artists" may be a legitimate empty result from Spotify's `time_range=short_term` constraint (needs recent ~4-week activity) rather than a bug — a possible follow-up (`medium_term`?) if the user wants it, not changed unilaterally.
> `SearchView.swift`'s `loadSpotify()` originally gated on `supabase.auth.currentUser?.appMetadata["providers"]` — a cached local snapshot, not a live check. The bounded retry (5 attempts, 600ms apart, from the earlier round) re-read that same frozen value every attempt, so if it was stale/incomplete on the very first read (plausible given this account's two-step signup: Spotify doesn't assert email_verified, so Supabase's confirmation-email step sits between the first OAuth attempt and the one that actually establishes a session), retrying could never self-correct. Fixed by switching to `supabase.auth.userIdentities()` (committed `606c0da`) — but a re-test still failed identically. Direct DB check (username `junnwest`) confirmed the identity link is real (`app_metadata.providers: ["spotify"]`, matching `auth.identities` row) — so the actual gap is one layer deeper: `SpotifyService.topArtists()`/`recentlyPlayed()`/`tokenIsLive()` discarded the real HTTP response on any failure, making an expired token, an insufficient-scope grant, a genuinely empty result, and a network failure all indistinguishable. Added `captureSpotifyAPIFailure()` (status code + truncated body → Sentry) to all three, plus a distinct event for "no provider token saved at all." **Next step needs either a Sentry dashboard check after the next test, or Sentry API access from this environment** — this round doesn't claim the bug is fixed, only that the next repro will finally say why.

> User reported the Universal Link built in the prior round (already committed, pushed, live) didn't actually open the app. Root cause, confirmed live: Supabase's confirmation email doesn't link straight to `sillajuku.com/auth/confirmed` — it links to `<project>.supabase.co/auth/v1/verify`, which server-side-redirects here. Universal Links only intercept a *direct* tap on a matching https:// link, never a page reached via a redirect chain — a structural limitation, not an AASA/entitlements misconfiguration (verified the deployed AASA file itself is correct and current). Fixed with the app's already-working custom scheme (`sillajuku://`, used for OAuth callbacks): `auth/confirmed/page.tsx` now auto-triggers `sillajuku://auth/confirmed` on mount (mobile only) plus a manual fallback link; `sillajukuApp.swift`'s `.onOpenURL` recognizes that path and posts the same `.sjEmailConfirmed` notification the Universal Link path already used. Also simplified the page's copy to the exact two lines requested, dropping the old mobile/desktop text branching. `tsc --noEmit` clean, iOS simulator build **BUILD SUCCEEDED**.

Added a third branch for `isAlreadyGranted` (authorized/provisional/ephemeral): shows "Notifications are already on" + a plain Continue, no pointless re-request. `StepAppleMusic.swift`: added a Spotify connect row (shown only if not already linked, via `supabase.auth.linkIdentity`), a one-liner explaining both connections only feed album suggestions, and a caption clarifying Continue can be declined safely. **Explicitly did NOT add a bypass button that skips the system MusicKit dialog** — confirmed with the user first, since that exact pattern got this screen rejected by App Review twice before (builds 9 and 11, Guideline 5.1.1(iv)); Continue's behavior is unchanged, only copy was added. > `DiscoveryViewModel.loadSpotify()` (`SearchView.swift`) made exactly one attempt to read the account's Spotify-linked status and provider token, both of which can still be settling right after a *brand-new* signup specifically — a single failed check gave up permanently, with the only recovery being to background/foreground the app or switch tabs (correctly rejected as unacceptable UX). Fixed by wrapping the existing logic (unchanged) in a bounded retry — up to 5 attempts, 600ms apart, ~3s worst case — re-checking both signals fresh each time rather than trusting one early read. Confirmed safe: every Add-tab section already renders independently and progressively, so in the rare race case only the Spotify row arrives a couple seconds later, nothing else is blocked. Production build clean.
