Distinct from the growth-content/data-insight thread directly below — don't conflate them.**
> 1. **USER ACTION NEEDED FIRST: paste `apps/web/supabase/migrations/20260919000000_ig_famous_artists.sql` into the Supabase SQL editor** (this project's convention — migrations are hand-applied, not pushed by scripts). Creates `ig_famous_artists` (watchlist) and `ig_detected_releases` (one row per detected release). Nothing in this pipeline can run until this lands.
> 2. **Design decision, confirmed live in this session by investigation, not assumption:** MusicBrainz's bulk date-window sweep (`discover-mb-newreleases.ts`'s technique, reused almost verbatim in the new `discover-ig-newreleases.ts`) is the PRIMARY detector here, with iTunes/Deezer used only for cover art — the reverse of the existing catalog pipeline's iTunes-first approach. Reason: the MB-lag problem that motivated the iTunes fast lane is specific to underground Korean acts (few editors); famous artists get same-day MB entries from any of many editors watching them.
> 3. **Also confirmed live this session: the EXISTING catalog recency pipeline (`recency`/`newreleases`/`gapfill` lanes) hasn't run at all in 18 days**, and even when running had real gaps (iTunes IP-blocks, ~19-day full-catalog rotation, 10,068 catalog artists with zero release_groups so recency can never seed off them). This is why the new pipeline is fully standalone rather than extending that one — not a comment on it needing a fix right now, just why it wasn't reused.
> 4. **New files, all untested against live data yet** (schema doesn't exist until step 1 above lands): `famous-artists-seed.ts` (rough starter list of ~70 globally famous artists, deliberately broad — edit freely), `resolve-famous-artists.ts` (name → MBID, "skip rather than guess" on ambiguous matches), `discover-ig-newreleases.ts`, `fetch-ig-cover.ts` (iTunes → Deezer → MB Cover Art Archive fallback chain), `draft-ig-caption.ts` (new OpenAI raw-fetch caption drafting — no prior pattern existed for this), `render-ig-poster.ts`, `run-ig-pipeline.ts` (orchestrates all four). All runnable individually via new `npm run ig:*` scripts, or together via `npm run ig:pipeline`.
> 5. **New dependency: Playwright**, added because no PNG rasterizer existed in the repo at all (`generate-trend-posters.ts` only ever outputs HTML, never rasterizes). Chromium already downloaded on this device (`npx playwright install chromium`, ~300MB) — **needs the same one-time install on the other device** before `render-ig-poster.ts` will run there.
> 6. **Ran the full pipeline live (2026-09-19):** all 77 seed artists resolved to MBIDs (48 auto, 29 hand-confirmed after the resolver correctly flagged them ambiguous — same-named unrelated MB entities; see SESSIONS.md for the full list). 21 real posters rendered into `scripts/output/ig-out-now/`. Caught and fixed 2 real bugs along the way: `release_date` needed to be `text` not `date` (MB gives year-only precision sometimes), and the poster's logo was a hand-drawn placeholder — replaced with the real `public/logo-text.svg` wordmark, recolored white via CSS fill inheritance.
> 7. **Scope changed mid-session, confirmed with the user:** famous watchlist artists should also become part of the REAL rating catalog (artists/release_groups/releases), not just tracked in the `ig_*` side tables — the catalog's Korean-underground focus is a resource constraint, not a permanent exclusion. Built `sync-ig-artists-to-catalog.ts`, reusing `ingestArtist()` (`mb-ingest.ts`) as-is — no new ingestion logic needed, since it's already idempotent/dedupe-safe for both brand-new and already-owned artists. **USER ACTION NEEDED: paste `apps/web/supabase/migrations/20260919000002_ig_detected_releases_catalog_synced.sql` into the Supabase SQL editor**, then run `npm run ig:sync-catalog:all` once to backfill all 77 current watchlist artists' full back catalogs (expect it to take a while — dozens of artists, some with large discographies capped at 800 release-groups each). `npm run ig:pipeline` now runs the incremental version of this automatically on every future run.
> 8. **Caption step dropped entirely.** User asked what `OPENAI_API_KEY` was for, learned ChatGPT Plus doesn't cover API access (separate product/billing), chose to skip captions rather than set up API billing. Deleted `draft-ig-caption.ts`, removed from `run-ig-pipeline.ts`/`package.json`; posters no longer gate on a caption.
> 9. **Poster logo fixed:** the placeholder flower+text mark rendered inconsistently (looked like a plus sign or a stray dot depending on the cover art behind it). Replaced with the real `public/logo-text.svg` wordmark, recolored white via CSS fill inheritance; all 21 already-rendered posters regenerated, not just future ones.
> 10. **Watchlist curation dashboard built, then rebuilt bigger after two real problems.** First version (110 hand-picked candidates) shipped as an Artifact with a `db` capability so picks persist — [https://claude.ai/code/artifact/1c05ccf5-4bec-4928-a013-2a3bd8fa9631](https://claude.ai/code/artifact/1c05ccf5-4bec-4928-a013-2a3bd8fa9631). **Problem 1:** the user's full review pass was silently lost — `window.claude.db` wasn't attached at the instant the page's top-level script ran, so every toggle looked like it worked but wrote nothing. Fixed with a retry loop, a loud failure banner (replacing an easy-to-miss grey status line), and a localStorage backup. **Problem 2:** the user then said 110 candidates was too small — rebuilt with **1,140 real candidates**: the mainstream 66 (Latin/Afrobeats dropped per the user's call) plus **1,075 real Korean hip-hop/R&B/indie/rock/singer-songwriter names pulled live from Wikipedia's category system** (`ko.wikipedia.org` `categorymembers` API — same technique already proven in the growth-content thread below, not hand-typed). Also had to switch candidate ids from array-position-based to a stable content hash (`stableId()` in `apps/web/scripts` build tooling, not committed — lives in the session scratchpad) — a position-based id would silently reattach a saved decision to the wrong artist on any future rebuild that reorders or adds candidates. **The user has NOT yet redone their review against this 1,140-candidate version** — that's the next concrete step once the incident below is resolved.
> 11. **🔴 CRITICAL, UNRESOLVED: running the `--all` backfill triggered a live Supabase incident.** `ig:sync-catalog:all` fully ingested 4 artists (Beyoncé, Kendrick Lamar, Ariana Grande, The Weeknd — genuinely in the catalog now) before throwing "timed out acquiring connection from connection pool" / connection-reset errors on Kanye West and Travis Scott. Stopped the script, but a direct test afterward showed the Data API and Metrics API returning **zero response at all** (not an error — a full hang) while the completely separate Auth service answered in 0.5s — a materially worse symptom than the project's prior 2026-09-02 Disk I/O Budget alert, where the Management API confirmed the DB stayed `ACTIVE_HEALTHY` and responding throughout. **As of this writing, recovery has NOT been confirmed by the user.** Do not resume `ig:sync-catalog:all` or run anything else DB-heavy until (a) the user confirms the Supabase dashboard shows the database responding again, and (b) the script has been redesigned to throttle between artists — 77 artists' full discographies (Beyoncé alone was 102 release-groups/599 recordings) back-to-back with zero pacing is what caused this.
> 12. **Remaining next-session work, in order:** ~~confirm DB health recovered~~ ✅ (2026-09-20, Mac — confirmed via live query checks, see below) → ~~redesign `sync-ig-artists-to-catalog.ts` with inter-artist delays/smaller batches~~ ✅ (2026-09-20, Mac — `INTER_ARTIST_DELAY_MS = 5000` added between artists) → **resume the backfill for the remaining 73 watchlist artists** (`npm run` script name TBD — check `package.json`'s `ig:*` entries) → user redoes the dashboard review against the 1,140-candidate list → sync `ig_famous_artists`/`famous-artists-seed.ts` to match the final picks → build the discussed-but-not-yet-built iTunes per-artist detection lane for niche Korean artists (MB's bulk sweep, the primary detector today, will under-detect them the same way it originally did for the main catalog — that's the whole reason the iTunes recency lane exists there) → `mb:audit` + `mb:gapfill` as final catalog-integrity/cover-art passes.
> 13. **2026-09-20 (Mac): the DB-unresponsive incident above recurred (or was still ongoing) — user reported the whole app/website non-functional, suspected another large data-collection batch.** Confirmed live: nearly every ordinary app query (feed, charts, search, rankings) was timing out (57014) or failing outright, one with a raw connection-level failure. Root-caused precisely (not guessed) by pulling this session's own incident notes above: the cause was **`sync-ig-artists-to-catalog.ts --all`** (this Instagram-watchlist backfill), not the regular MusicBrainz catalog pipeline — independently confirmed idle/not-running (18+ day stale heartbeats) at the time, so it couldn't have been the cause. **Fixed**: added a 5-second delay between each artist in the sync loop, directly targeting the diagnosed mechanism (zero pacing between large-discography artists never gave Supabase's connection pool room to recycle). Re-checked live afterward — the database had already self-healed (same pattern as prior incidents) once the runaway process stopped; ordinary queries are back to 200-600ms. **User was also advised, separately**: this class of incident is a workload/connection-pool-capacity issue, not evidence Supabase itself is unreliable — recommended checking the current Supabase compute add-on size and considering a read replica to isolate future batch work from live traffic, rather than migrating database vendors.

> **▶ IN PROGRESS (2026-09-20, Mac) — iOS: fixed a real TestFlight bug where "Create a username" during onboarding could spin forever with no error, blocking signup. NOT YET COMMITTED. Full detail in SESSIONS.md (2026-09-20).**
> User reported a friend stuck on this step, plus a separate Sentry `pkceGrantCodeExchange` alert around the same time — investigated and determined the two are unrelated (the Sentry error's `4/0A` code prefix is Google's OAuth format, not Apple's; Apple Sign-In on iOS uses a direct ID-token exchange with no PKCE step at all, so it structurally can't produce that error). The real bug: `StepUsername`'s availability check (`Onboarding/StepGenre.swift`) had no timeout and, on any failure, silently reset to a neutral state with no error message or retry — a hung request left the spinner running forever with Continue permanently disabled. Fixed with a 10s timeout race (`withThrowingTaskGroup`, since the Supabase client has no built-in per-call timeout) and a real inline error + Retry button, matching the step's existing "username taken" error style. Also tightened a related latent race (a stale/cancelled check clearing the spinner out from under a newer one) while touching the same function. Could not live-verify against an actual network failure this round — production build clean, standard/low-risk Swift concurrency patterns.
>
> **▶ IN PROGRESS (2026-09-11, Mac) — iOS Taste tab rebuilt as a full-screen vertical pager (Reels/Shorts-style), replacing the single long scroll it shared with web. NOT YET COMMITTED. Full detail in SESSIONS.md (2026-09-11).**
> 1. **`TasteView.swift`'s `TasteReportView`** now renders through a new `TastePagerContainer` — one full-bleed, swipe-to-advance page per section (hero, map, #1 album, by-the-numbers, years, score, scene, canon, standings, disliked, outro), snapped via iOS 17 `.scrollTargetBehavior(.paging)`, with a tappable trailing dot rail (`ScrollViewReader.scrollTo` for the jump) and a light haptic on every settled page change. The "You vs the community" section became its own horizontally-swipeable one-genre-per-card carousel (was a long vertical dumbbell list) so it fits one screen. Light haptics also added to the HallOfFame ring's front-album changes and the stat-tile/dumbbell tap-to-reveal tooltips.
> 2. **Found and fixed a real, non-obvious SwiftUI bug along the way**, not just built the shell: jumping to a page several screens away (e.g. tapping a dot near the end) landed mid-page instead of at its top, worse the farther the jump — a `GeometryReader` sizing each page reports its *safe-area-excluded* size while `.scrollTargetBehavior(.paging)` snaps against the view's *full* bounds, a small mismatch that compounded every page. Fixed by reading the true full bounds (`.ignoresSafeArea()` on the reader itself) and re-adding status-bar/home-indicator clearance manually. See the code comment on `TastePagerContainer.body` for the full note — worth reading before touching this file again.
> 3. **Round 2, live user feedback ("doesn't feel optimized for mobile")** — the first pass still read as a small white "card" floating in a mostly-empty screen (`TastePageChrome`'s `Color.sjSurface`-boxed content, centered with big flanking `Spacer`s). Removed the boxed-card chrome entirely — content now sits directly on the page's own cream canvas, the same layering every other screen in this app already uses — and scaled up the pages that read as sparse once un-boxed: hero's aurora wash now fills the whole screen (was a small rounded-rect card) with much bigger title/stat typography, the Canon gauge grew 112→180pt, HallOfFame covers 184→208pt, stat tiles/activity chart/standings cards all got bigger type and more padding. Two components (`StatTileView`, the 12-month-activity panel) had their own `Color.sjCream` fill swapped to `Color.sjSurface` — they used to contrast against an outer *white* card; without that card they'd have blended invisibly into the now-cream page.
> 4. **Live-verified in the Simulator**, not just compiled: built a temporary `#if DEBUG` mock-data harness (bypassing the auth gate + `TasteViewModel` with a fabricated `TasteProfileResponse`) to screenshot every page without a real signed-in 25+-rating account — used across both rounds, confirmed the pager-jump fix and then the full-bleed redesign, fully removed before finishing each time. `git diff` on `sillajukuApp.swift` is clean, only `TasteView.swift` changed.
> 5. **Round 3, live user feedback again — two more fixes:**
>    - **Fixed a real HallOfFame-ring gesture-conflict bug** (user: "the carousel in section 2 is weird"). `HallOfFameView`'s ring used a plain `DragGesture` with no axis lock, attached via `.gesture(...)` — since this ring now lives on a page inside the vertically-paging `ScrollView`, that gesture could claim any touch starting inside its hit box, including a vertical page-swipe, stalling it. Web sidesteps the equivalent conflict with CSS `touch-action: pan-y` (confirmed by reading `page.tsx`'s `HallOfFame`, no SwiftUI equivalent exists). Fixed with `.simultaneousGesture` (so it never exclusively claims the touch away from the ScrollView) plus an explicit `abs(translation.width) > abs(translation.height)` guard so a vertical swipe never spins the ring even briefly.
>    - **Added the wrap-around loop the user asked for**: swiping past the outro page (matching its own "swipe up to loop back to the top" copy) now plays a flat, taste-viz-colored bubble-rise transition that fills the screen, then cuts to page 0 underneath while fully covered. Implemented as one more row appended to `TastePagerContainer` (`loopsAround: true`) reached by a completely normal forward swipe — deliberately not a custom overscroll-gesture, to avoid the exact class of bug just fixed above. New `LoopTransitionView`.
> 6. **Round 4, live user feedback again — four more fixes, one of them a real regression this session's own earlier work introduced:**
>    - **🐛 Fixed a tab-bar overlap bug** (user: "the first section overlaps with the bottom navbar") — every mock-data verification up to this point used a bare `NavigationStack`, never the real `MainTabView`/`TabView`, so this never showed up until now. Root cause: `TastePagerContainer`'s full `.ignoresSafeArea()` escapes *every* ambient safe-area context, not just the device notch/home-indicator it was meant to bleed under — including the bottom inset `MainTabView`'s own `TabView` adds for its tab bar.
>    - **That fix (trying `.ignoresSafeArea(edges: .top)` instead of the full version) silently reintroduced the pager-jump drift bug from item #2 above** — confirmed by re-testing the same far-page jumps that bug was originally fixed against. Root-caused for real this time with the "measure-then-ignore" pattern: an *outer*, non-ignoring `GeometryReader` reads the true local safe-area insets (which correctly include the tab bar height, real automatic SwiftUI propagation, nothing bespoke), wrapping an *inner* `GeometryReader` that carries the actual `.ignoresSafeArea()` for the paging math and uses those measured insets to pad manually. Re-verified across 5 pages (0, 3, 5, 7, 9) plus the wrap-around loop, all clean, tab bar never overlapped.
>    - **Added drag-to-scrub on the dot rail** (user ask: hold and drag the dots to navigate quickly, instead of one swipe per section). `PageIndicatorDots` now tracks a single `DragGesture(minimumDistance: 0)` over the whole rail, mapping the finger's vertical position to a page index continuously — doubles as the existing tap-to-jump for free. Added a basic `.accessibilityAdjustableAction` too, since replacing per-dot `Button`s with one gesture would otherwise have quietly dropped VoiceOver support.
>    - **Deleted the outro page** ("That's your taste snapshot") per direct request — the wrap-around loop (`loopsAround`) now appends its trigger row after whichever page ends up last for a given profile, no explicit hand-off page needed.
>    - **Reworked the loop transition** (user: "the bottom to top transition is a bit off and unnatural") — it used to be one page's own content, a bouncy spring, and a silent cut with no visible reveal (the "reveal" literally happened on a row already scrolled off-screen, so the user never saw it). Now a fixed overlay (`LoopBubbleField`) independent of scroll position: bubbles rise with a plain `easeOut` (no spring overshoot), fully cover the screen (including behind the tab bar — a real full-bleed wipe), the pager jumps back to page 0 *underneath* while still covered, then the *same* overlay recedes to reveal it — a real, visible reveal instead of a hidden swap.
>    - Live-verified all four in the Simulator with the same temporary mock-harness approach, this time wrapped in a genuine multi-tab `TabView` specifically so the tab-bar bug couldn't hide again; fully removed before finishing (`sillajukuApp.swift` diff clean, `TasteView.swift` grepped for leftover scaffolding).
> 7. **Round 5, live user feedback again — the tab-bar fix from round 4 had its own side effect**: "the first page's content is wrapped ... I see white in the top and bottom." Root cause: fixing the tab-bar overlap by padding every page's content by the real safe-area insets was applied *outside* each page (wrapping the whole row), which also shrank the hero page's own full-bleed aurora background away from both edges, leaving a gap. Fixed by moving that padding *inside* each page instead of around it — `TastePagerContainer`'s `content` closure now hands each page its own `(topInset, bottomInset)` to apply itself (`TastePageChrome` adds it to its existing title padding; the hero page insets only its *text content*, letting its `.background()` stay full-bleed). Re-verified: hero's aurora now reaches every edge (under the status bar, behind the tab bar) and a non-hero page (09) still shows no drift — the tab-bar and drift fixes from round 4 both still hold.
> 8. **Round 6, live user feedback again — "the carousel still looks odd."** Since the round-3 gesture fix couldn't be verified by feel, asked what specifically was still off rather than guessing (`AskUserQuestion`) — got three concrete, distinct answers, all fixed:
>    - **🐛 A real animation bug**: `HallOfFameView.ringCard` drove opacity/dimming/border/shadow off a discrete `isFront` boolean (`index == round(turn)`) while the actual rotation angle is continuous — so those four properties would all snap instantly the moment the front index ticked over mid-transition, exactly when a card is passing through edge-on (already thin from the 3D rotation itself). Matches the user's description exactly: "covers 1 and 3 exist... for less than a second as a vertical line." Fixed by driving all four from the already-continuous `depth = cos(rad)` value instead — a smooth `frontAmount` (0...1) everywhere, no discrete flip. `circularDistance()` is now unused and was deleted.
>    - **🐛 The "grey border beneath the album cover"**: every cover carried a permanent drop shadow, including a constant `opacity 0.10` shadow on every *non-front* card at rest — not a bug exactly, but confirmed live to read as an unwanted border rather than a depth cue. Softened substantially and made continuous with `frontAmount` (front: ~0.14 opacity/8pt radius, back: ~0.04 opacity/3pt radius, down from a flat 0.10/5pt).
>    - **Swipe conflict, still reported after the round-3 fix**: bumped the ring's `DragGesture` `minimumDistance` 4→10pt, giving an intended vertical page-swipe more room to declare itself before the ring's gesture engages at all, on top of the existing axis check. Can't be fully confirmed without a physical device either way.
>    - Live-verified the shadow softening and multi-cover geometry (bumped the mock to 4 tied albums for this), then removed the harness again.
> 9. **Round 7, live user feedback again — "i still see the vertical lines... let's make the other covers invisible once it's not on spotlight."** Round 6's continuous-opacity fix removed the *discrete pop* but left a floor: `opacity = 0.22 + 0.78×frontAmount` never actually reaches zero, so the edge-on/thin-line moment was still dimly visible at 22%. Per the user's own suggested direction: cubed `frontAmount` (`spotlight = frontAmount³`) instead of a linear floor — a steep falloff that reaches (imperceptibly close to) full transparency well before a cover reaches edge-on, so only the current front cover and whichever neighbor is actively swinging into/out of it are ever visibly on screen. Applied to opacity, dim, border, and shadow uniformly. Re-verified: at rest, only the front cover renders, no visible fragments of neighbors.
> 10. **Round 8, live user feedback — page 01 (the taste map), not the ring this time: "the transitions on click is a bit awkward. could have more interactive UI."** `TasteMapView.swift` (`Components/`, not `TasteView.swift`) had three concrete gaps: drilling into a world's sub-genres (and backing out) was a flat `.opacity` cross-fade with no sense of depth; rows used `.buttonStyle(.plain)`, which gives zero visual response to a touch until the tap's *result* appears; and the whole map had never gotten the haptics pass every other Taste-tab interaction got this session. Fixed all three: drill-in/back now use a directional `.asymmetric` slide+fade (forward drills in from the trailing edge, back exits the same way in reverse — tracked via a new `navigatingForward` flag), each row got a custom `RowPressStyle` (light tint + slight scale on press), and `Haptics.selection()`/`.light()` now fire on world drill-in, back, and opening a sub-genre's sheet. `Haptics` itself changed from `private` to internal (`TasteView.swift`) so a second file could use it, rather than duplicating it. Live-verified the base (undrilled) state renders correctly with the new press style and `.clipped()` (added to contain the slide) — the animation/press-feel itself, like every other interaction fix this session, can't be confirmed via a static screenshot.
> 11. **Round 9, live user feedback again — "the last to first slide transition is a bit off."** Diagnosed: `LoopBubbleField` never actually traveled — bubbles only scaled 0.001→1 in place (a symmetric bloom-then-shrink), never matching the original "bubbles coming up" request or giving the reveal any real direction. Rebuilt around a 3-phase `LoopBubblePhase` enum (`below` → `inPlace` → `above`) driving a real `.offset(y:)` in addition to the existing scale/opacity: bubbles now genuinely rise in from below the screen to fully cover it, hold while the pager jumps to page 0 underneath, then continue rising up and off the top — one continuous upward motion revealing the hero page, rather than a cover that just blooms and un-blooms in place. Live-verified with a rapid Simulator screenshot burst (the whole cycle completes in ~1.3s, faster than expected) — caught all three phases: full bubble coverage, and the rise-off mid-reveal with the hero page's title genuinely visible sliding out from underneath the receding bubbles.
> 12. **Round 10, user idea: replace the loop transition's flat bubbles with the actual flower-mark halftone texture.** The flower logo (`logo-flower.svg`) turned out to be a real CMYK print halftone — cyan/magenta/yellow dot grids at classic offset screen angles (15°/75°/0°) plus a sparse black key layer at 45°, multiply-blended, measured directly off the asset via connected-component centroid analysis (confirmed near-exact match to the standard print convention). Prototyped as a web artifact first (side-by-side texture comparison + a phone-frame preview) before touching Swift. Follow-up ask refined the mechanic twice: smaller dots, and — instead of one rigid image sliding — every dot ignites/extinguishes individually from its own fixed position, biased toward igniting bottom-up (with jitter so the frontier reads as scattered ink, not a ruled line) and extinguishing bottom-up again using the *same* per-dot thresholds, so cover and reveal are one continuous bottom-up motion rather than a mirror. Ported into `TasteView.swift`: `LoopBubblePhase`/`LoopBubbleField` deleted outright, replaced by `HalftoneLoopOverlay` (`TimelineView(.animation)` + `Canvas`, the Apple-recommended pattern for procedural per-frame drawing that isn't just interpolating one SwiftUI layout property) plus a small `HalftoneDot` model and a deterministic `SeededRNG` (mulberry32) so the jitter doesn't reshuffle on every rebuild. `runLoopTransition` now just starts a timestamp and lets the overlay time itself, jumping the pager back to page 0 partway through on the same schedule as before. Live-verified with a Simulator screenshot burst: caught full-screen coverage matching the real logo's texture almost exactly, and a mid-uncover frame showing dots cleared at the bottom while still dense at the top — confirming the bottom-up direction works as asked.
> 13. **Round 11, user ask: "the last page to first page scroll has to be more resistant and less smooth than the other scrolls."** Every page-to-page swipe used the same built-in `.scrollTargetBehavior(.paging)`, so the swipe that triggers the wrap-around loop felt exactly as easy/light as any other page turn — nothing made it read as a distinct, harder-to-trigger gesture. New `LoopGuardedPaging: ScrollTargetBehavior` replaces `.paging` on the Taste pager: it reproduces standard nearest-page snapping (including honoring a decisive flick, same heuristic `.paging` uses) for every ordinary transition, but the one swipe from the last real page onto the invisible loop-trigger row requires the drag to cross 82% of a full page before it commits — short of that, it springs back to the last page instead of advancing. Verified the dot-rail's `jump(to:)` (used by tap/drag-scrub navigation) goes through `ScrollViewReader.scrollTo` directly, not this target-computation path, so it's unaffected — only a real in-progress finger-drag gesture is gated. Confirmed the app still builds and the pager still renders normally; the resistance itself, like every other gesture-feel change this session (the ring drag, the dot-rail scrub), can only be judged with an actual finger on an actual screen, not a Simulator screenshot.
> 14. **Round 12, user ask: "the scroll shouldn't be continuous... a single vertical swipe should go to the section right below and snap almost immediately," plus "add more haptics in every interaction."** Root cause of the first part: `LoopGuardedPaging` (round 11) rounded to the nearest page off the *system's own momentum-projected target*, which for a hard flick can land two or three pages away — so one strong swipe could glide straight past several sections instead of landing on the very next one, reading as continuous rather than discrete. Rewrote it to cap every swipe to exactly one page (`fromPage` or one beyond it, direction only, magnitude discarded) regardless of how far the projection would otherwise reach — true one-swipe-one-page paging, matching `UIScrollView.isPagingEnabled`. This also produces "snap almost immediately" as a direct consequence: the system now only ever has to animate at most one page's distance to settle, never several. The velocity-based "decisive flick" branch was dropped entirely in the same pass (not just left in) — the system's proposed target already bakes release velocity into its distance, so a simple magnitude-discarding threshold on that alone reproduces the same feel without needing to guess `context.velocity`'s sign convention, which had never been exercised live anyway (Simulator can't inject touch).
>     - **Haptics audit across the whole tab found four real gaps with zero feedback**: the hero page's Refresh button, the error state's Retry button, the lock screen's "Find releases to rate" CTA, and the album/rec rows inside the taste-map's sub-genre sheet (`NavigationLink`s with no tap feedback at all) — all four now fire `Haptics.light()`. Two new, purposeful haptics added alongside: `Haptics.rigid()` (new case on the enum, sharper than `.medium`) fires exactly when the loop swipe's resistance gate blocks an under-committed drag — a "hit a wall" bump reinforcing the resistance round 11 built; `Haptics.medium()` fires once the loop transition actually commits, a confirming pulse for the single most novel interaction in the tab.
>     - Verified the app builds and the pager still renders correctly post-change; the one-page cap and the new resistance-bump haptic are both drag-physics behaviors that, like every gesture-feel change this session, can't be exercised by a Simulator screenshot — only reasoned about from the code.
> 15. **Scope was explicit, confirmed with the user up front (`AskUserQuestion`):** build the full paging shell + light interaction upgrades now; deepen each section's interactions individually in a follow-up pass. **Open for next session:** that deepening pass, a real-device check (Simulator + screenshots only so far — the ring gesture fix, the drag-scrub dots, the taste-map transitions/press-feedback, the halftone overlay's exact feel/timing, and now the one-page-cap paging + resistance haptic in particular can only be truly confirmed by an actual finger on an actual screen), whether the still-generous empty space above/below centered content on sparser pages (numbers, standings) needs further work, and deciding whether to commit.
> 16. **Round 13, bug report: "sometimes the scroll from taste pages' final section to first section doesn't work (after the transition comes an empty screen)."** Root cause: nothing stopped the user from swiping *again* while the halftone overlay was mid-cover — `HalftoneLoopOverlay` ignores hit testing (it has to, it's decorative), so every touch during the ~1.3s covered window passed straight through to the still-fully-interactive `ScrollView` underneath. A stray swipe or dot-rail tap during that window could drag the real scroll position away from page 0 right as (or after) `runLoopTransition`'s own `scrollTo(0)` set it there, sometimes leaving the pager stuck on the invisible, `Color.clear` loop-trigger row once the cover receded — a real empty screen, intermittent because it only happened when a swipe landed at the wrong moment. Fixed with `.scrollDisabled(isLooping)` on the pager's `ScrollView` and `.allowsHitTesting(!isLooping)` on the dot rail — both block user-driven interaction for exactly the transition's duration while leaving `runLoopTransition`'s own programmatic `scrollTo` untouched (`scrollDisabled` only disables gesture-driven scrolling, not `ScrollViewReader.scrollTo`). That distinction was the one thing worth verifying live rather than assuming, since getting it backwards would've made the bug permanent instead of fixing it: triggered the transition via a debug harness and confirmed it still lands cleanly on the hero page every time, not the empty row.
> 17. **Round 14, user ask: "make the haptic of the final scroll (last to first section) more dramatic."** Was a single `Haptics.medium()` fired the instant the transition committed, nothing else. Replaced with a deliberate two-beat arc: `Haptics.heavy()` (new case on the enum) at commit — a stronger, more decisive thud than `.medium`, the payoff for pushing the swipe past the resistant gate — then `Haptics.success()` (new case, `UINotificationFeedbackGenerator`'s distinctive multi-pulse pattern, not another single impact) once the reveal actually finishes and the hero page is back on screen. Two distinct beats — "it's happening" then "you've arrived" — rather than one flat pulse louder. Build succeeds; a pure haptic change has nothing to screenshot, and the Simulator produces no Taptic Engine output at all to play back, so this one is unverifiable by any means short of a physical device — flagged plainly rather than claimed as tested.
> 18. **Round 15, user ask: "in the loading screen, make the wordmark logo black instead of gray."** Both loading screens' `logo-text` image had `.opacity(0.5)` stacked on top of `.foregroundStyle(Color.sjInk)` — the near-black ink color was there, just washed out to 50% by the modifier sitting right below it. Removed `.opacity(0.5)` from both `LaunchLoadingView` and `AppLoadingView` (the cold-launch and in-tab loading screens made static two rounds ago). Live-verified via the same debug harness pattern as that round: the wordmark now renders solid black, not gray. Debug scaffolding removed and grepped clean afterward.
> 19. **iOS build number bumped 17 → 18** (`CURRENT_PROJECT_VERSION`, both Debug and Release configs in `sillajuku.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj`).
> 20. **Round 16, user report: round 13's loop-scroll fix "did not fix yet"** — a blank page after the transition, requiring a scroll-up-then-down-again to recover. Round 13's own verification turned out to be flawed: it called `runLoopTransition` directly from a resting page-0 state, which could never exercise the one thing that actually matters — the scroll view's state right after *arriving* at the guarded row via a real swipe. Rebuilt the debug harness to animate-scroll into the guarded row first (mimicking what a real gesture settle leaves behind) before letting the transition fire naturally — a meaningfully different, more faithful test than before, even though it still can't inject a real touch. Root-caused round 13's own `.scrollDisabled(isLooping)` fix as the most likely culprit: toggling a live `ScrollView`'s own scroll-enabled state in the same instant a real user gesture might still be mid-deceleration is a plausible way to freeze its content offset somewhere other than the intended target, distinct from — and unlike — a `ScrollViewReader.scrollTo` call issued from an already-settled (non-gesture) position, which is all round 13's test ever actually proved safe. Replaced three separate flags (`scrollDisabled` on the ScrollView, `allowsHitTesting(!isLooping)` on the dot rail, `allowsHitTesting(false)` on the overlay) with one: the halftone overlay itself is now hit-testable exactly (and only) while it's on screen, shielding the entire pager underneath from stray touches without ever touching the ScrollView's own enabled state. Also swapped the reset's `disablesAnimations` transaction for a short real animation (0.15s, fully hidden behind the ~260ms opaque hold) — the same animated-`scrollTo` code path `jump(to:)` already uses successfully elsewhere, rather than animation-suppression machinery whose interaction with a just-settled gesture was never confirmed either. Re-verified with the improved harness: still lands cleanly on the hero page. Flagged plainly to the user that this remains unverifiable by touch in the Simulator — the fix is reasoned from the most plausible mechanism, not proven against the actual reported repro.
> 21. **Round 18, user report: still broken — "scrolling at the final tab of taste leads to a blank page on first scroll."** "On first scroll," not "sometimes," was the key new detail — pointed at something structural, not a rare race. Re-examined the whole mechanism from scratch instead of patching further: `onScrollVisibilityChange(threshold: 0.6)` — the signal that fired `runLoopTransition` — fires as soon as the invisible loop-trigger row crosses 60% visible, which for a real finger-drag swipe happens well *before* release, not after. `runLoopTransition` was starting the cover/reset sequence while the user's finger could still be down and the gesture still live, racing the reset against whatever the still-active drag did next. Realized every prior verification (rounds 13 and 16) used `scrollTo`/`jump()` to arrive at the guarded row, never a real touch — meaning this exact scenario, an active gesture still in flight when the trigger fires, had never once been exercised by any test run so far, which is exactly why two "fixes" in a row failed to change anything. Replaced the visibility-based trigger with a **debounced** one off `currentPage` landing on the guarded row: every change to `currentPage` restarts a 220ms wait, and the real transition only commits once the position has genuinely stopped changing — a signal that doesn't depend on knowing exactly when `currentPage` updates relative to touch-up (continuously during a drag, or only after settling; genuinely unclear and unverifiable here either way), because it's robust to both. The invisible row's `onScrollVisibilityChange` was removed entirely, no longer needed. Re-verified with the debug harness: still lands cleanly on the hero page. Said plainly to the user again that this is reasoned from the most plausible mechanism given everything read so far, not proven against a real touch — that gap can't be closed from this environment.
> 22. **Round 19, three explicit layout asks across three Taste-tab sections in one message.**
>     - **"08 You vs the community: remove the horizontal swipe and display everything at once."** `StandingsCarousel` (a one-genre-per-card horizontal paging carousel, dot counter, per-card haptic) replaced outright with `StandingsList`: every genre's comparison card stacked vertically inside its own `ScrollView(.vertical)`, no paging state, no dots. Nested same-axis inside the outer pager's own vertical `ScrollView` — this coordinates through standard nested-scroll delegation (inner list scrolls until it can't, then the pager's swipe takes over), unlike the orthogonal horizontal-vs-vertical conflict the carousel was originally built to dodge.
>     - **"merge 07 into 06."** Canon reach (page 07, a radial gauge + one sentence, no gating condition of its own) folded into "Where your music comes from" (page 06): scene breakdown, a divider, a small "CANON REACH" label, then the gauge, all on one page. Page numbers shift down automatically (`buildPages`' numbering is a running counter over whichever sections are actually present, not hardcoded). **Found and fixed a real bug this surfaced, not by inspection but by testing**: the merged page rendered completely blank on first try. Root cause: `TastePageChrome`'s content wrapper centers its content vertically (`Alignment.leading` — leading-horizontal, *center*-vertical) rather than pinning it to the top; every existing page happened to fit within one screen, so this was never visible before, but the merged (now taller) content overflowed the available height and centering pushed it off *both* the top and bottom edges at once, rendering nothing. Fixed by wrapping the merged content in its own bounded `ScrollView` (same pattern as the other two fixes this round) rather than changing `TastePageChrome`'s alignment globally, which risked shifting every other page's layout for a problem only this one page actually has.
>     - **"01 Taste map: ...set a maximum height of the list and make the user scroll within the wrapped region... fix the position of the go back button to below the header with a suitable padding."** `TasteMapView`'s row list (worlds, or a world's sub-genres once drilled in) previously had no height cap at all — a longer list just kept growing the page's total content height, crowding `RampLegendView`'s score gradient up against the tab bar. Wrapped the row list in `ScrollView(.vertical).frame(maxHeight: .infinity)` inside the existing outer VStack, so the back button and legend keep their natural size and the list absorbs whatever space is left, scrolling internally past that. Back button: added `.padding(.top, 12)` to the view's outer VStack -- previously it (or the row list itself, when no button was showing) sat flush against the page's lead text with no gap at all.
>     - Live-verified all three, including the blank-page bug and its fix, with a debug harness carrying deliberately oversized mock data (14 taste-map worlds, one with 8 sub-genres, 12 standings rows) specifically chosen to exceed one screen -- the original 2-world/1-tag mock could never have surfaced the overflow bug at all. Debug scaffolding removed and grepped clean afterward, `sillajukuApp.swift` diff confirmed to hold no leaked markers, full production build re-run clean.
> 23. **Round 20, user ask: "make each section sharable. make a share button on the top right of the screen. same as sharing a post -- make it sharable as instagram story primarily and provide other options as well."** Researched the existing share pipeline before writing anything (`InstagramShare.swift`, `SharePreviewSheet.swift`, `ShareCardView.swift`, all four existing call sites) rather than building a second, parallel share flow: the Instagram-Stories pasteboard handoff, compositing, Save to Photos, and the native share sheet were all already fully generic (`UIImage` in, `UIImage`/`Data` out) — only `SharePreviewSheet` itself was coupled to the specific "rating card" shape (`PendingShare`/`ShareCardView`), and even there, everything *except* the two lines that construct `ShareCardView` had zero knowledge of what the sticker actually contained.
>     - **Generalized `SharePreviewSheet` additively, not by rewriting it** — `pending: PendingShare` became `pending: PendingShare?` plus a new `customCard: AnyView?`, with a second initializer (`init<Content: View>(@ViewBuilder card:)`) alongside the original. All four existing call sites (`AlbumContextMenu`, `AlbumDetailView` ×2, `MixLibraryView`) needed zero changes — confirmed via `git diff --stat` showing no modifications to any of them. The "Blurred Cover" background option is hidden for custom cards (`availableStyles` filters it out) since there's no single cover image to blur behind arbitrary content.
>     - **One `TasteShareCard` wrapper for every section, not nine bespoke redesigns** — added to `TastePageChrome` itself (the shared chrome every section but the hero already renders through), so a share button + `.sheet` + card-building logic added *once* automatically covers every section that uses it. The card reuses each section's own already-built `content` closure verbatim inside a fixed-width, sillajuku-branded frame, rather than re-implementing each section's visuals a second time. The hero page (not wrapped in `TastePageChrome`) got the same button and mechanism added directly, with its own compact stat restatement (`heroShareContent`) since its full aurora-background layout is built for a full screen, not a card.
>     - **Fixed-height content area (420pt, `.clipped()`), not `maxHeight: .infinity`** — `TasteMapView`'s and `StandingsList`'s internal `ScrollView`s (both added earlier this session) size themselves relative to whatever height their parent proposes; inside an `ImageRenderer` snapshot with no bounding `GeometryReader` ancestor, an unbounded proposal would have rendered each list's *entire* unscrolled content at whatever height that turned out to be, not a consistent story-shaped card.
>     - **Found and fixed two snapshot-specific bugs by testing, not by inspection:** (1) the hero share card's stat row reused `heroStat`/`CountUpText`, which animates from 0 over ~0.9s on appear — since `SharePreviewSheet` snapshots within a frame or two of appearing, this would very likely have exported cards reading "0 rated / 0 genres / 0 worlds"; replaced with a plain static `Text` version (`heroShareStat`) for the card specifically, live page unaffected. (2) Three charts (`StackedBarView`, `ScoreRampChartView`, `ActivitySparkView`) grow their bars in only once a real `onScrollVisibilityChange` fires — which never happens inside a static snapshot, so every bar would have exported at zero height. Added a `isTasteShareSnapshot` environment key (set by `TasteShareCard`, read by all three views alongside their existing `UIAccessibility.isReduceMotionEnabled` check — the same "skip the animation, show the end state" precedent already established for accessibility) rather than restructuring each chart's animation logic.
>     - Live-verified three structurally different sections (hero stats, the taste map's internal-scroll list, the scene+canon merged bar-chart-and-gauge page) with a debug harness, confirming the generalized sheet, the fixed-height crop, and both snapshot fixes all together — including a title-wrapping bug caught in the same pass (`TasteShareCard`'s title truncated to an ellipsis in the narrower card width; fixed with `.fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)`, same fix already used for `TastePageChrome`'s own lead text elsewhere in this file). Debug scaffolding removed and grepped clean afterward, full production build re-run clean.
> 24. **Round 21, user question then fix: "why does white render as grey? or is it actually gray?"** (about the share sheet's first "Solid Color" swatch). Wasn't a bug — that swatch was `creamColor`, `Color(red: 0.973, green: 0.973, blue: 0.961)` (~#F8F8F5, the app's fixed brand cream, deliberately not the adaptive `Color.sjCream` so an exported story never shifts with the phone's dark mode), genuinely just off-white by design. Explained why it *read* as gray anyway: a ~7-point RGB difference from pure white is easy to miss in isolation, but a 24pt swatch circle with a thin border sitting directly on the sheet's true-white background makes that subtle warmth read as dingy via simultaneous contrast. User asked for it to just be white — changed the first `solidPresets`/`gradientPresets` entries and the default `solidColor`/`gradientTop` state from `creamColor` to `Color.white` (all three needed updating together, since the default state has to match a preset for the selection ring to show correctly on first appearance). Left `creamColor` itself and its other two call sites (the "Blurred Cover"/"Photo & Video" styles' fallback background when there's no image/media yet) untouched — those are a different, unrelated use of the same constant, not what was reported. Live-verified: the swatch and canvas now render genuine white, indistinguishable from the sheet's own background except for the selection ring. Debug scaffolding removed and grepped clean afterward, full production build re-run clean.
> 25. **Round 22, immediate follow-up: "not only the background, i think the sticker should be white as well."** The sticker (the draggable card itself, `TasteShareCard`) had its own separate `.background(Color.sjCream)`, untouched by round 21's canvas-only fix — same family of "off-white reading as gray" issue, just on the card instead of the canvas behind it. Changed to `Color.white`. Live-verified against a gradient canvas specifically (temporarily defaulted the debug preview to the gradient style so the now-white sticker would actually be visually distinguishable from the background behind it, rather than testing white-on-white and being unable to tell if anything had changed) — confirmed the card now renders genuine white, clearly separated from the blue gradient behind it. Debug scaffolding, including the temporary gradient-default override, fully removed and grepped clean afterward, full production build re-run clean.
> 26. **Round 23, user ask: "make the share buttons liquid glass — like the notification button in the home."** Matched `HomeView.swift`'s `bellButton` pattern exactly: `Circle().fill(Color.clear).glassEffect(.regular, in: Circle())` via `.background { ... }`, the same iOS 26 Liquid Glass convention already used by `MorphingRateButton`, `ScoreBadge`, `FlowerRateControl`, and other buttons across the app. Removed `TasteShareButton`'s old manual capsule/stroke styling; changed icon `foregroundStyle` from `Color.sjBlue` to `Color.sjInk` to match the bell exactly. Live-verified against two different backgrounds (hero's aurora gradient, taste map's flat cream) via debug harness — glass circle reads correctly on both. Debug scaffolding removed and grepped clean afterward, full production build re-run clean.
> 27. **Round 24, real bug report: "for section 01, the main content gets eliminated in the instagram app, even though it shows as expected in the preview."** Root cause, confirmed with an isolated `ImageRenderer` repro before touching real code: `ScrollView` (backed by `UIScrollView`) renders as entirely blank when snapshotted by `ImageRenderer` with no window ancestor — the in-app preview looked fine because it's a live, window-attached view, but the raw image actually handed to Instagram goes through a window-less `ImageRenderer` and came out blank wherever a section's content used a `ScrollView`. Fixed in all three affected sections (`TasteMapView`, `StandingsList`, and a new `SceneCanonContent` extracted from the merged scene+canon page) by swapping to a plain `VStack` — still bounded by the existing fixed-height `.clipped()` frame — whenever `isTasteShareSnapshot` is true, since scrolling has no meaning in a static export anyway. `SceneCanonContent` had to become its own `View` type rather than an inline branch, since only a distinct type re-reads the environment separately at each of its two embed sites (live page vs. share card). Re-verified the real `TasteMapView` fix end-to-end against production code (mock taste-graph data through the actual `TasteShareCard`/`ImageRenderer` pipeline) — rows, covers, share bars, and score badges all now export correctly. Debug scaffolding removed and grepped clean afterward, full production build re-run clean.
> 28. **Round 25, user ask: "i wish the scrolling motion of the taste page is much faster... similar to Instagram Reels and YouTube Shorts."** `LoopGuardedPaging` already caps *where* a swipe lands (at most one page away); this round targets *how fast* it visibly gets there, which SwiftUI's `ScrollView` has no public modifier for — that's the underlying `UIScrollView.decelerationRate`. Added `FastPagingDeceleration`, an invisible `UIViewRepresentable` inside the pager's `ScrollView` that finds the real `UIScrollView` via its `superview` chain and sets `decelerationRate` to a custom `0.85` (snappier than even the system's own `.fast` preset of 0.99). Build is clean and the change is layout-inert. **Could not live-verify the felt result this round** — driving an actual swipe gesture into the Simulator needed GUI automation (`cliclick`) that wasn't registering taps at all, likely a permissions gap outside the code itself. Flagged to the user to try by hand; `0.85` is the one constant to retune if it doesn't feel right.
> 29. **Round 26, immediate follow-up: "when i swipe slowly the motion alse becomes slow. it should be quick no matter what speed the user swipes as long as it is classified as a swipe."** Round 25's fix was insufficient in principle, not just under-tuned: native `UIScrollView` paging derives the settle *animation's duration* from release velocity regardless of `decelerationRate`, so a slow release still glides slowly across the same one-page distance — a real characteristic of native paging, and exactly why Reels/Shorts don't use it. Confirmed the bigger fix with the user first (it touches the pager's core gesture handling), then implemented it: `LoopGuardedPaging.updateTarget` now cancels native momentum outright once a page change is decided, and drives the move itself via `UIView.animate(withDuration: 0.22, ...)` on the real `UIScrollView`'s `contentOffset` — fixed duration, independent of swipe speed. Reused all of the existing target-decision logic (one-page cap, loop-guard resistance) unchanged; only what drives the final visible motion changed, so nested scroll coordination (`TasteMapView`/`StandingsList`'s own internal lists) and dot-rail/haptic state tracking are untouched. Build is clean. **Could not live-verify again** — GUI-automated taps still weren't registering in the Simulator. Flagged to the user directly; `0.22`s is the one number to retune if the settle timing feels off.
> 30. **Round 27, real bug report: "the swipt speed hasn't fixed yet... it should be uniformly rapid."** Found the actual reason round 26's fix never ran at all: `PagerScrollViewFinder` was placed as a `.background{}` on the `ScrollView` itself, which SwiftUI composites as a *sibling* of the real `UIScrollView`, not a descendant inside its scrollable content — walking `superview` from a sibling can never reach it, so `scrollViewBox.scrollView` was `nil` every single time, silently falling back to fully native paging on every swipe for both rounds 25 and 26. Fixed by moving the finder inside the `ScrollView`'s own content. Also discovered `cliclick` taps actually *do* work in this environment once the Simulator-window-to-device-point coordinate mapping is calibrated correctly — last round's "permissions gap" conclusion was wrong, it was a calibration failure. Verified the fix directly with a data-free debug harness (`TastePagerContainer` needs no real Taste data): confirmed the finder now locates a real `UIScrollView` (`HostingScrollView`). Also found, via a temporary log inside `updateTarget`, that `cliclick`'s simulated drag gestures never actually trigger it at all — so while the root-cause bug is confirmed fixed with real evidence, the actual settle-speed-uniformity still needs the user's own hands-on swipe test. Debug scaffolding fully removed and grepped clean, production build re-run clean.
> 31. **Round 28, different area, user ask: "browsing around the add tab lags a lot. is there something expensive in this page?"** Research agent traced it to `SearchView.swift`'s `discoveryView` (the Add tab's default landing content): its ~7 horizontally-scrolling rows (Recently Listened, From Your Taste, For You, each genre-cluster row, Popular, New Releases, Trending) used plain `HStack` instead of `LazyHStack`, building every album card in every row immediately on a cold visit rather than lazily as scrolled into — a confirmed divergence from `HomeView.swift`, which already uses `LazyHStack`/`LazyVStack` correctly for the same kind of row. Fixed with a 4-line diff: `LazyHStack` in `spotifyArtistScroll`, `appleMusicArtistScroll`, and `albumScroll` (reused ~7×), plus `LazyVStack` on `discoveryView`'s outer container — matching the existing `HomeView.swift` pattern exactly. Lower verification bar than the pager fixes above: Lazy containers are drop-in visual equivalents inside a `ScrollView` (same layout, different build timing), not a novel technique, so a clean production build was sufficient; didn't additionally screenshot the Add tab since the simulator's signed-in session was lost after an earlier round's reboot and re-establishing it needed a real Spotify OAuth flow, not attempted.
> 32. **Round 29, severe regression report: "the scroll doesn't work. i only can see the first page and cannot move on."** Round 27's manual `UIView.animate` hijack (meant to make settle speed independent of swipe speed) fully broke the pager: it told SwiftUI's native paging "nothing moved" (`target.rect.origin.y = fromY`) while separately driving the real `UIScrollView.contentOffset` by hand — SwiftUI's `scrollPosition(id:)` binding believed the old position was still correct and kept re-asserting it, fighting and beating the one-shot manual animation every time, so every swipe snapped back to page 0. **Fixed by reverting to reporting the real target truthfully** (`target.rect.origin.y = page * pageHeight`) — a literal revert to the exact line that worked correctly for ~15 rounds before round 26, not a new mechanism. Kept the one-page-cap/loop-guard logic (never implicated) and `decelerationRate = 0.85` (a safe property that doesn't fight SwiftUI's reconciliation) as the one remaining, safe partial speed mitigation. Also removed `PagerScrollViewBox` and simplified `PagerScrollViewFinder` — with the manual-animation code gone, the box/state-holder indirection built solely to feed it was dead weight. **Full swipe-speed-independent settle timing is no longer being pursued** — the one approach that achieved it is what broke the feature, and restoring function took priority. Production build clean.
> 33. **Round 30, immediate follow-up: "the uniform speed is broken again. fix it again with another approach that will not cause the bug."** Found the actual missing piece from round 29's failure: `jump(to:proxy:)` (dot-rail taps' proven, never-buggy navigation) directly writes `currentPage` *and* calls `scrollTo` — round 26/27's broken attempt only mutated raw `UIScrollView.contentOffset`, never touching `currentPage`, so SwiftUI kept correcting the scroll back to the stale state. Fix: `LoopGuardedPaging` gained an `onCommit` closure wired to `{ page in jump(to: page, proxy: proxy) }` — the identical, already-proven call dot-taps make — instead of touching `UIScrollView` directly. Since `jump` writes `currentPage` itself, there's nothing left for the binding to fight, structurally, not just by luck. Given this is the second regression report on this exact bug class, verified the shared mechanism directly via a data-free debug harness: repeated dot-tap jumps across non-adjacent pages (0→4→2) all landed correctly with no reversion — confirms the `jump`/`currentPage`/`scrollTo` path `onCommit` now delegates to is robust. Had to fully recalibrate the Simulator-window coordinate mapping again (it shifts across simulator restarts, not stable to assume). Debug scaffolding removed and grepped clean, production build clean.
> 34. **Round 31, immediate follow-up: "this is too fast. make it a bit looser."** Feel tweak, not a bug fix — `jump(to:proxy:)`'s settle animation (shared by dot-tap, drag-scrub, and swipe-triggered transitions alike) changed from `.easeInOut(duration: 0.3)` to `.spring(response: 0.4, dampingFraction: 0.8)`, a spring's natural give reading as "looser" more convincingly than just a longer fixed ease would. Kept as one shared curve across all three trigger methods rather than a swipe-only value, so the settle never feels inconsistent depending on how it was triggered. Pure animation-parameter change on the same already-proven-safe mechanism from round 30 — nothing structural touched. Production build clean.
> 35. **Round 32, immediate follow-up: "still too fast. i can't see the swiping motion."** Round 31's first bump was too small to read as visible motion. Moved response further this time (0.4 -> 0.7, dampingFraction 0.8 -> 0.75) rather than repeating a timid increment. Same shared curve across swipe/dot-tap/drag-scrub. Pure parameter change on the unchanged jump mechanism. Production build clean.
> 36. **Round 33, two requests: spring response "let's try 1.5" + a real visual bug: swiping up from the hero section shows a hard border between its blue aurora gradient and the next section's plain cream.** Response bumped 0.7 -> 1.5, no other change there. For the seam: root-caused to the hero's third aurora glow being centered almost exactly at the page's bottom edge (y: 0.96), so it's still near full strength right where the page clips, with nothing on the next page to bridge into. Fixed by adding a matching top-anchored fade (same aurora color, fading to clear over 200pt) to whichever page follows the hero, targeted generically by pager index rather than a hardcoded section name. Verified visually with an isolated repro at real screen proportions — the hard edge is gone, reads as one continuous wash into cream. Debug scaffolding removed, production build clean.
> 37. **Round 34, immediate follow-up: "make it 2.5."** Spring response bumped 1.5 -> 2.5 on the same jump mechanism. Production build clean.

> **▶ IN PROGRESS (2026-09-20, Mac) — Web: fixed `/api/charts/silla` intermittently failing (~48.6% error rate), root cause of a Vercel "function duration spike (9.2x)" alert. Migration APPLIED (user ran it in the Supabase SQL editor, confirmed successful) — pending a redeploy + a post-deploy check that the error rate actually dropped. Full detail in SESSIONS.md (2026-09-20).**
> User reported the Vercel alert, then pasted the Functions dashboard table, which pointed straight at `/api/charts/silla`'s error rate (every other route was 0%). Root cause, confirmed live (reproduced the exact `57014` statement-timeout error via a direct RPC call): `get_silla_leaderboard`'s `user_stats` CTE recomputes AVG/STDDEV/COUNT(score) grouped by user_id over the entire `ratings` table, on every single call — even though it's completely unfiltered (doesn't depend on genre/country at all), and `ratings` has no index on `user_id`, so it's a full scan + sort every time. This was already a known ~7s cost when the route's caching layer was built (see the route's own code comment); the catalog/ratings tables have only grown since, pushing it over both the anon role's 3s timeout and, intermittently, the route's own service-role budget.
> **Fix** (new migration `20260920000000_silla_leaderboard_user_stats_cache.sql`): moved the redundant, unfiltered per-user stats out of the per-request path into a small precomputed table (`user_score_stats`), refreshed on a schedule (new cron `/api/cron/refresh-silla-stats`, wired into `vercel.json` daily at 16:00 UTC, an hour before the existing Spotify-taste cron) instead of recalculated on every cache-miss request. `get_silla_leaderboard` now reads from that table via a plain JOIN instead of an inline `GROUP BY` over all of `ratings`. Deliberately left `global_mean` (the other CTE touching all of `ratings`) as a live query — it's a single-pass `AVG`, not the bottleneck, and switching it to an average-of-per-user-means would subtly change the Bayesian prior's math (equal-weight-per-user vs. equal-weight-per-rating), a behavior change this fix isn't meant to make. The migration's last line runs the refresh immediately so the very first post-migration call already benefits, rather than waiting for the first cron tick.
> **Applied by the user directly** — no `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` in this environment's `.env.local`, so the migration couldn't be run from here (this project's established convention is hand-applying migrations via the Supabase SQL editor anyway); the user pasted the file into the SQL editor and ran it, confirmed by the SQL editor's own output (`refresh_user_score_stats` returning an empty cell — the correct, successful result for a `void`-returning function, not an error). `tsc --noEmit` clean on the new cron route. **Still needed: a redeploy** so `vercel.json`'s new cron entry actually takes effect, and a follow-up check of the Vercel Functions tab to confirm `/api/charts/silla`'s error rate actually dropped post-fix.

> **✅ SESSION CLOSE (2026-09-02, Windows, continued) — a monitor notification caught LIVE DATA CORRUPTION on a real artist ($NOT) within minutes; root-caused, fixed, cleaned up, blast-radius audited. While restarting to ship that fix, found a SECOND, more serious bug: the new Spotify recency lane (built earlier the same session) was actively degrading production Spotify search for real users. Both fixed, both verified live.**
> 1. **🐛🚨 Data corruption: `pickAlbum()` in `itunes-client.ts` had a title-only fallback (zero artist check) that let 28 releases from an unrelated iTunes catalog attach to the real artist "$NOT."** A short/generic title of his ("SIMPLE," "0%," "DRIP") collided with an unrelated instrumental-covers catalog's identically-named albums, and the fallback accepted it blind. Shared by `discover-itunes-recency.ts`, `mb-gapfill.ts`, `discover-itunes-backfill.ts`, `audit-caa-covers.ts`, `verify-coverage.ts` — pre-existing, not new this session. **Fix:** added a loose-but-nonzero artist check to the fallback (mirrors the existing `titleArtistOk()` pattern from `backfill-rg-covers-all.ts`). Verified directly: `SIMPLE`/`0%` by `$NOT` now correctly find nothing; `DRIP` by `$NOT` still correctly finds the real single.
> 2. **Cleaned up + audited before declaring done.** Deleted the 28 bad release groups + releases (0 ratings existed on them — checked first). Then checked whether the bug did *more* damage than just $NOT: 0 further suspects across the other 64 `[itunes]` release groups created this session (including the other artists flagged in the same notification window — Ungu, Harry Stone, Nino Segarra, Antoon, Nadhif Basalamah, Sillkey — all legitimate) and 0 suspects across 600 `gapfill`-checked groups (via the raw iTunes track-artist name trail). One honest caveat: 44 gapfill cover-only fills this window have no raw-artist trail left to check retroactively — can't be fully verified after the fact, though the collision mechanism is inherently rare and is now fixed going forward.
> 3. **🐛🚨 Second bug, found mid-restart: `recency-spotify` never checked the shared production Spotify circuit breaker before calling Spotify, and had no request spacing at all** (unlike the iTunes client). It burst through a batch with zero delay, tripped a real ~18.8-hour Spotify penalty, and kept calling anyway — actively prolonging a block the **live web server's own Spotify search fallback reads from the same Redis key.** Confirmed directly (`spotify:rate-limited-until` read back ~18.8h remaining). **Fix, three parts:** request spacing added to `spotify-client.ts` (mirrors `itunes-client.ts`'s existing delay); the shared circuit now checked before every Spotify call, short-circuiting to no-op if open; `pipeline.ts` also checks it before claiming a batch (separately necessary — the lane's cursor advances per artist unconditionally, so claiming a batch while blocked would silently skip-scan and permanently lose that slice of the sweep).
> 4. **Verified live**: `pipeline:status` now shows `recency-spotify blocked (shared circuit) ... done=0 «~1125m remaining»` — correctly refusing to touch Spotify or advance its cursor. The 18.8h block itself is real Spotify-side state that just has to expire; the fix only stops us from re-triggering or prolonging it further.
> 5. **Restarted the pipeline three times this session** for these two fixes (`--fresh-focus --no-embed --no-qc`) — freshness/gapfill/recency(iTunes)/reconcile all carried over cleanly each time. `gapfill`/`recency`(iTunes) currently show `blocked (iTunes 403)` — unrelated, self-heals in 120m, just heavy iTunes traffic from this incident's own investigation.
> 6. **Still open:** confirm `recency-spotify` resumes normally once the shared Spotify circuit clears (~2026-09-03 ~10:39 UTC) — worth a quick check next session. The lesson generalizes: any *new* external-API script lane needs both per-request spacing AND an explicit shared-circuit check before consuming cursor/queue state — the iTunes lane had the first from day one, which is likely why only the fresh-this-session Spotify lane had the gap.
>
> **✅ SESSION CLOSE (2026-09-02, Windows) — Supabase Disk IO Budget warning arrived mid-session, root-caused and mitigated; the iTunes recency bridge turned out to have been SILENTLY BROKEN since the first time it ever completed a full sweep, found and fixed; then a SECOND, independent Spotify recency bridge was built alongside it after the user pushed back on relying on MB+iTunes alone — proven live with real hits within the same session, including a genuine hang bug caught and fixed at pipeline scale, not just in testing.**
> 1. **Disk IO:** Supabase emailed that project `bside` is depleting its Disk IO Budget. Traced it to `qc`'s `integrityCheck()` — meant to run once an hour, but because it kept timing out *before* reaching that sleep, the supervisor's 30s crash-restart made it retry the same expensive full-table scan roughly every 30-60s instead, continuously, since the previous restart. **`qc` is now disabled** (`--no-qc`) — freshness/gapfill/newreleases/recency all unaffected, no work lost. Supabase doesn't expose the actual IO-budget percentage via API, so this couldn't be directly confirmed as *the* cause, only *a* clear, real, wasteful one — worth checking whether the warning recurs.
> 2. **🐛 The big one: `recency`'s cursor query has been permanently stuck at "0 ingested" since the first time it ever wrapped around.** `id` is a `uuid` column; the loop's own `.gt('id', after)` throws Postgres `22P02 invalid input syntax for type uuid: ""` when `after` is the empty string — which it always is right after a full sweep completes. The code never checked the query's `error`, so a failed query silently read as "swept the whole set, 0 to ingest" — and since that branch re-saves the cursor as `''` again, **every attempt after the first wrap hit the identical error forever.** This was NOT introduced this session — the empty-cursor state is reached at the end of every normal sweep, so this had likely been quietly doing nothing for a long stretch of this lane's life. Fix: only apply `.gt('id', after)` when non-empty, check `error` explicitly. **Live-confirmed within minutes of the fix**, not just typechecked: first real batch surfaced genuine MB-missing releases for **Matheus & Kauan** (Brazilian, +8) and **DJ CHARI** (Japanese, +5) — both non-Korean, both proof the lane is doing real work again.
> 3. **Recency bridge ALSO widened from KR-only to catalog-wide** (`scripts/pipeline.ts` recencyLoop + `scripts/discover-itunes-recency.ts`), prompted by the user listing 11 real recent releases to check — 9 of 11 turned out to not be in MusicBrainz at all yet (confirmed live against MB's API, a genuine cataloging lag, not our bug), but the KR-only `.eq('country', ...)` filter was ALSO silently excluding Korean artists with a null `country` value (SUPERBEE, Rosy Pereira — a data gap) and structurally excluded every non-KR artist (Fat Joe, Lil Wayne, Jay Worthy) from any MB-lag mitigation at all. Dropped the filter; each artist's own `country` now feeds the iTunes search-language hint instead of one fixed value for the whole batch. Batch/interval bumped 5-per-30min → 25-per-10min so sweeping ~68k artists (vs. ~12k KR-only before) still finishes a full cycle in ~19 days. `tsc --noEmit` clean.
> 4. **The user pushed back: "if MB and iTunes don't contain 9 of the releases, we shouldn't be relying on them" — checked empirically, then built a second source.** Spot-checked those 9 releases against Spotify directly: **6 of 9 were there, same-day** (Northfacegawd, rosy, SUPERBEE, Jay Worthy, Fat Joe, Lil Wayne). Built a full second recency bridge — `scripts/spotify-client.ts` (cooperates with the existing production-shared Spotify rate-limit circuit), `scripts/spotify-ingest-core.ts` (dedicated Spotify writer, reuses iTunes-core's source-agnostic utilities), `scripts/discover-spotify-recency.ts` (same dedup-safe scan logic) — wired in as a new `recency-spotify` pipeline lane alongside (not replacing) the iTunes one, independent cursor/circuit so a block on one never stalls the other. `reconcile-itunes-mb.ts` already handled `source='spotify'` — zero changes needed there.
> 5. **🐛 Found a genuine hang bug at pipeline scale that CLI testing hadn't caught.** `spotify-client.ts`'s `fetch()` calls had no timeout — the lane's second batch sat stuck for 20+ minutes on one stalled connection, and this lane (like the iTunes one) has no watchdog to force a restart. Fixed with `AbortSignal.timeout(15000)` on every Spotify call. **Live-confirmed immediately**: the very next batch found 13 real releases in one pass (BUNT. ×5, Phantogram, Vicente Fernández ×6, Hologram Lo').
> 6. **Verified the write path end-to-end before trusting the pipeline run**: a live `--ingest` test on SUPERBEE, then a direct DB read confirming "82 life flow (feat. dj wegun)" — the exact track from the user's original 11 — is now genuinely in the catalog with correct cover art, credits, and tracklist.
> 7. **Still open:** neither `recency` nor `recency-spotify` has watchdog protection (unlike `ingest`/`freshness`) — worth adding if a real unrecoverable hang ever shows up despite the new fetch timeouts. Jay Worthy is "resolved" in our DB but was never actually scheduled for ingestion (`next_check_at: null`) — a separate small gap, not fixed this session. 희대의/도모, kii kii/WhyKiiKii, and 반타01/아름8 aren't in MusicBrainz, iTunes, **or** Spotify — nothing on our end can collect these until some source catalogs them.

> **✅ SESSION CLOSE (2026-08-25, research only, no code) — sillajuku passed App Store review and is awaiting release. User asked whether release timing affects long-term App Store discoverability, and what to do about wanting to release to friends now while still polishing before a "real" launch. Researched (web search across ASO industry sources + Apple developer forums), no code touched. Full detail in SESSIONS.md.**
> - **Decision context for whoever picks this up:** no evidence that a quiet friends-only release now creates any lasting penalty later — App Store ranking is continuously recalculated off rolling retention/engagement/rating signals, not a fixed launch-day score (Apple's own dev forums call the "day-1 downloads set your ceiling forever" idea an outdated myth). The one real cost of releasing quietly is forfeiting the "new app boost" (~7-day organic keyword-search visibility bump, one-time per country) — but that's irrelevant right now since friends install via direct link, not App Store search, so there's nothing to lose by using it now vs. later.
> - **Recommendation given, not yet acted on:** release to friends now under the current build; treat the "real" public launch (ASO — keywords/screenshots/Custom Product Pages, review-seeding, any community/press push) as a **separate, later, deliberately-timed event** once the planned pre-launch improvements are done — mirrors the professional soft-launch-small-then-coordinated-hard-launch pattern found in the research, not something specific to this app.
> - **No numeric study exists** tying "captured the new app boost" to success odds, or quantifying how much harder a delayed public push is — flagged honestly rather than invented. Base-rate context surfaced instead: ~0.4-0.7% of apps hit $100K/12mo revenue (GoPractice study), unconditional on launch timing.
>

> **✅ SESSION CLOSE (2026-08-18, Mac, launch loading screen) — User: "loading screen: create a percentage gauge + raise error if device is not connected to network." Scoped to the app-launch splash specifically (user's explicit choice over the Home-feed splash) — `RootView`'s `.loading` case in `sillajukuApp.swift`, shown while `observeAuth()` waits for Supabase's first auth event. That case was previously just `Color.white.ignoresSafeArea()` — a blank screen with nothing on it, which is also the one place a disconnected device could get stuck indefinitely (this auth check needs network for anything beyond a cached signed-out state).**
> 1. **New `Services/NetworkMonitor.swift`** — `@Observable` singleton wrapping `NWPathMonitor`, push-based (`isConnected` flips automatically the instant the path changes, no polling). Starts optimistic (`true`) so it doesn't flash a false error before the first path update arrives.
> 2. **New `LaunchLoadingView`** (private struct, bottom of `sillajukuApp.swift`) replaces the blank white case: same flower/wordmark branding as `MainTabView`'s existing `AppLoadingView`, plus a percentage-ring gauge — reuses the app's own established ring pattern from `ScoreBadge` (`Circle().trim(from:to:).stroke(..., lineCap: .round).rotationEffect(-90°)`), filled via a time-based ease (`0.92 * (1 - exp(-t/1.4))`) since there's no discrete multi-step progress to measure here, just one opaque wait — deliberately capped short of 100% so it never "lies" about being done before the real transition happens. Swaps to a "No internet connection" notice (with `wifi.slash` icon) the instant `NetworkMonitor.shared.isConnected` goes false; no retry button needed since the monitor is push-based and the underlying auth check never stopped running — reconnecting flips it back on its own.
> 3. **Live-verified in Simulator via a precisely-timed screenshot burst** (auth resolves in ~150-250ms with a cached session, too fast for a single fixed-delay screenshot to reliably catch) — caught the gauge mid-render showing a live "0%", confirmed the transition into the (unchanged) Home-feed `AppLoadingView` afterward still works. **Not live-tested: the actual offline/no-network state** — cutting real network to the Simulator would mean disabling the Mac's own WiFi, too disruptive to do mid-session. The code path (`NWPathMonitor` → `@Observable` property → `if/else` in the view body) is standard, well-precedented SwiftUI/Network-framework usage, but flagging this honestly rather than claiming a live-verified offline test that didn't happen.
> 4. **Out of scope, untouched:** `MainTabView.swift`'s `AppLoadingView` (Home-feed loading, post-auth) — user explicitly chose the launch splash only when asked which of the two loading screens this should apply to.
> 5. **`xcodebuild -scheme sillajuku -destination 'generic/platform=iOS Simulator' build` → BUILD SUCCEEDED.** Committed + pushed (`90affeb`).
